## GHSA-r2v3-8gwf-7ghm · github.com/bank-vaults/vault-secrets-webhook

2026-07-31 · Go · **CRITICAL** 9.6 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The webhook allowed the Vault address to be specified via an object annotation (common.VaultAddrAnnotation) without proper validation. This meant an attacker could control the URL to which the webhook made an outbound HTTP call, leading to Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF). Additionally, the `vault_skip_verify` annotation could be set by an object, allowing an attacker to bypass TLS certificate verification.

### Impact

An attacker could force the webhook to make requests to arbitrary internal or external network resources, potentially accessing sensitive internal services, cloud metadata endpoints, or other systems. This could also lead to the theft of Kubernetes ServiceAccount tokens via the TokenRequest API.

### Key diff

```
--- a/pkg/webhook/webhook.go
+++ b/pkg/webhook/webhook.go
@@ -201,6 +214,14 @@ func (mw *MutatingWebhook) newVaultClient(ctx context.Context, vaultConfig Vault
 		return nil, clientConfig.Error
 	}
 
+	// Validate before any connection or ServiceAccount token mint.
+	if vaultConfig.AddrFromObject {
+		if err := common.ValidateObjectAddr(vaultConfig.Addr, vaultAddrPolicy()); err != nil {
+			vaultAuthAttemptsErrorsCount.WithLabelValues("config_error").Inc()
+			return nil, errors.Wrap(err, "rejected Vault address from object annotation")
+		}
+	}
+
 	clientConfig.Address = vaultConfig.Addr
 
 	tlsConfig := vaultapi.TLSConfig{Insecure: vaultConfig.SkipVerify}
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new `AddrFromObject` field to track if the Vault address originated from an object annotation. It also adds a `vaultAddrPolicy` function and `common.ValidateObjectAddr` to validate the Vault address against an allowlist and private IP ranges if it comes from an object annotation. Furthermore, `vault_skip_verify` is now controlled by a new `vault_allow_object_skip_verify` configuration, preventing arbitrary bypass of TLS verification.

## GHSA-6h5j-32cf-4253 · apostrophe

2026-07-31 · JavaScript · **CRITICAL** 9.1 · Pattern: `PROTOTYPE_POLLUTION→OVERRIDE`

### Root cause

The `apos.util.set` function in ApostropheCMS did not properly validate dot-separated paths provided by users. This allowed attackers to use special path segments like `__proto__`, `constructor`, or `prototype` to modify the global object prototype, leading to prototype pollution.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve process-wide authorization bypass by polluting the prototype of objects, potentially altering application logic or security-sensitive configurations for all users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/apostrophe/modules/@apostrophecms/util/index.js
+++ b/packages/apostrophe/modules/@apostrophecms/util/index.js
@@ -35,6 +35,13 @@ const util = require('util');
 const { stripIndent } = require('common-tags');
 const glob = require('../../../lib/glob.js');
 
+// Dot-path segments that must never be traversed when walking a
+// user-supplied path in `apos.util.get` and `apos.util.set`. Following any
+// of these reaches the prototype chain and enables server-side prototype
+// pollution (CWE-1321), e.g. a PATCH `$pullAll` key of
+// `__proto__.publicApiProjection`.
+const unsafePathSegments = new Set([ '__proto__', 'constructor', 'prototype' ]);
+
 module.exports = {
   options: {
     alias: 'util',
@@ -819,6 +830,12 @@ module.exports = {
           }
         }
         path = path.split('.');
+        for (p of path) {
+          if (unsafePathSegments.has(p)) {
+            // Refuse to write through the prototype chain (CWE-1321)
+            throw self.apos.error('invalid', `Unsafe property name "${p}" in dot path`);
+          }
+        }
         for (i = 0; (i < (path.length - 1)); i++) {
           p = path[i];
           o = o[p];
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a `unsafePathSegments` set to explicitly block access to `__proto__`, `constructor`, and `prototype` when using `apos.util.get` and `apos.util.set`. It also includes fixes for SSRF by preventing URL construction from attacker-controlled `Host` headers and XSS by properly escaping editor-controlled variables in script tags.

## GHSA-wf43-fpp3-cf65 · @apostrophecms/seo

2026-07-31 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 8.7 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from multiple issues: unsanitized user-controlled input (Google Analytics/GTM ID) being directly interpolated into script tags, an attacker-controlled Host header being used to construct an internal proxy URL, and a lack of validation for dot-path segments allowing prototype chain traversal.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary JavaScript into web pages, leading to session hijacking, data theft, or defacement (Stored XSS). They could also redirect internal requests to arbitrary hosts (SSRF) or manipulate object prototypes to potentially bypass security checks or cause application instability (Prototype Pollution).

### Key diff

```
--- a/packages/seo/lib/nodes.js
+++ b/packages/seo/lib/nodes.js
@@ -214,14 +214,21 @@ function getMetaHead(data, options) {
     });
     nodes.push({
       name: 'script',
-      body: [ {
-        raw: `
+      body: [
+        {
+          raw: `
   window.dataLayer = window.dataLayer || [];
   function gtag(){dataLayer.push(arguments);}
   gtag('js', new Date());
-  gtag('config', '${global.seoGoogleTrackingId}');
-`
-      } ]
+  gtag('config', `
+        },
+        { json: global.seoGoogleTrackingId },
+        { raw: ');\n' }
+      ]
     });
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the XSS by using a JSON node to safely escape user-controlled IDs within script tags. The SSRF is fixed by ensuring the internal proxy URL is resolved against the configured base URL rather than the raw Host header. Prototype pollution is prevented by explicitly disallowing '__proto__', 'constructor', and 'prototype' segments in dot-path traversals.

## GHSA-6x26-6r6f-m537 · thumbor

2026-07-31 · Python · **HIGH** 8.2 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→REGEX`

### Root cause

The `ALLOWED_SOURCES` configuration in Thumbor was intended to restrict image loading to specific domains. However, it treated string patterns as unescaped regular expressions by default. This allowed an attacker to use regex wildcards (like a dot '.') to bypass hostname restrictions, matching unintended domains.

### Impact

An attacker could bypass the intended domain restrictions and force Thumbor to load images from arbitrary external hosts, potentially leading to Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF) or other content injection attacks.

### Key diff

```
--- a/thumbor/loaders/http_loader.py
+++ b/thumbor/loaders/http_loader.py
@@ -61,9 +65,13 @@ def validate(context, url, normalize_url_func=_normalize_url):
             pattern, Pattern
         ):
             match = url
-        else:
+        elif looks_like_legacy_allowed_source_regex(pattern):
+            warn_legacy_allowed_source_regex(pattern)
             pattern = f"^{pattern}$"
             match = res.hostname
+        else:
+            pattern = f"^{re.escape(pattern)}$"
+            match = res.hostname
 
         if re.match(pattern, match):
             return True
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `http_loader` to explicitly escape string patterns in `ALLOWED_SOURCES` before using them as regular expressions, unless they are already compiled regex objects or contain explicit regex markers. It also introduces a warning system for legacy string-based regex patterns to encourage migration to compiled regex objects.

## GHSA-mw3h-qjxj-6xg9 · thumbor

2026-07-31 · Python · **HIGH** 8.2 · Pattern: `MISSING_VERIFICATION→SIGNATURE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability stemmed from an insecure way of removing the URL signature prefix using multiple `.replace()` calls. If the URL contained a crafted string that mimicked the signature prefix multiple times (e.g., `//signature//signature/image.jpg`), the `.replace()` method would remove all occurrences, potentially stripping more of the URL than intended. This could lead to a different URL being validated by the HMAC than the one actually requested, allowing an attacker to bypass signature validation.

### Impact

An attacker could craft a malicious URL that would appear valid to the Thumbor server, even without knowing the secret key. This could allow them to access or generate unauthorized images, potentially leading to denial of service, resource abuse, or other unintended image manipulations.

### Key diff

```
-            url_to_validate = url.replace(
-                f"/{self.context.request.hash}/", ""
-            ).replace(f"/{quoted_hash}/", "")
+            url_to_validate = self._strip_url_signature_prefix(
+                url, self.context.request.hash, quoted_hash
+            )
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new static method `_strip_url_signature_prefix` that explicitly checks if the URL starts with a known signature prefix and, if so, removes only that single prefix. This prevents the issue where multiple occurrences of the signature string within the URL could be removed, ensuring that only the intended signature prefix is stripped before HMAC validation.

## GHSA-mx5j-mp4f-g8jg · savon

2026-07-31 · Ruby · **HIGH** 8.1 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→COMMAND`

### Root cause

The Savon::Model library dynamically defined methods for SOAP operations using `module_eval` with a string interpolated from user-controlled WSDL operation names. This allowed an attacker to inject arbitrary Ruby code into the method definition string, which would then be executed when the method was called.

### Impact

An attacker could achieve arbitrary code execution on the server by crafting a malicious WSDL operation name that, when evaluated, would execute their desired Ruby code.

### Key diff

```
-      class_operation_module.module_eval %{
-        def #{StringUtils.snakecase(operation.to_s)}(locals = {})
-          client.call #{operation.inspect}, locals
-        end
-      }, __FILE__, __LINE__ - 4 # -4 points to the line where the eval string starts
+      method_name = operation_method_name(operation);
+
+      class_operation_module.define_method(method_name) do |locals = {}|
+        client.call operation, locals
+      end
```

### Fix summary

The patch replaces the use of `module_eval` with `define_method`. Instead of building a string that is then evaluated, it directly defines methods using a symbol for the method name and a block for the method body, preventing any code injection through the operation name.

## GHSA-vvp7-h4fj-m28w · github.com/gtsteffaniak/filebrowser/backend

2026-07-31 · Go · **HIGH** 7.7 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The application allowed authenticated users to request subtitle and lyrics files using a 'name' parameter for subtitles and a 'path' parameter for lyrics. The previous implementation used `idx.GetRealPath` which, while attempting to resolve paths within a user's scope, was not sufficiently robust against path traversal sequences in the 'name' or 'path' parameters, allowing access to arbitrary files outside the intended directory.

### Impact

An authenticated attacker could read arbitrary files on the server's filesystem by manipulating the 'name' parameter in subtitle requests or the 'path' parameter in lyrics requests to include path traversal sequences (e.g., `../../`).

### Key diff

```
--- a/backend/http/media.go
+++ b/backend/http/media.go
@@ -37,58 +36,49 @@ func subtitlesHandler(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request, d *requestContext)
 	name := r.URL.Query().Get("name")
 	embedded := r.URL.Query().Get("embedded") == "true"
 
-	userscope, err := d.user.GetScopeForSourceName(source)
+	fileInfo, err := files.FileInfoFaster(utils.FileOptions{
+		FollowSymlinks:           true,
+		Path:                     path,
+		Source:                   source,
+		Expand:                   true,
+		Content:                  false,
+		Metadata:                 true,
+		ExtractEmbeddedSubtitles: config.Integrations.Media.ExtractEmbeddedSubtitles,
+		ShowHidden:               d.user.ShowHidden,
+		HideFileExt:              d.user.HideFileExt,
+		SkipExtendedAttrs:        false,
+	}, store.Access, d.user, store.Share)
 	if err != nil {
-		return http.StatusForbidden, err
+		return errToStatus(err), err
 	}
-
-	idx := indexing.GetIndex(source)
-	if idx == nil {
-		return http.StatusNotFound, fmt.Errorf("source %s not found", source)
+	if !strings.HasPrefix(fileInfo.Type, "video") {
+		return http.StatusNotFound, fmt.Errorf("file is not a video")
 	}
 
-	realPath, _, err := idx.GetRealPath(userscope, path)
-	if err != nil {
-		return http.StatusNotFound, fmt.Errorf("file not found: %v", err)
+	track := findSubtitleTrack(fileInfo.Subtitles, name, embedded)
+	if track == nil {
+		return http.StatusNotFound, fmt.Errorf("subtitle track '%s' not found", name)
 	}
 
-	parentDir := filepath.Dir(realPath)
 	var content string
-
 	if !embedded {
-		content, err = utils.GetSubtitleSidecarContent(filepath.Join(parentDir, name))
+		subtitlePath := filepath.Join(filepath.Dir(fileInfo.RealPath), filepath.Base(track.Name))
+		content, err = utils.GetSubtitleSidecarContent(subtitlePath)
 		if err != nil {
 			return http.StatusInternalServerError, fmt.Errorf("failed to get subtitle sidecar content: %v", err)
 		}
 	} else {
-		// For embedded subtitles, we need to find the stream index by name
-		// Get file modification time for caching
-		fileInfo, err := os.Stat(realPath)
-		if err != nil {
-			return http.StatusInternalServerError, fmt.Errorf("failed to stat file: %v", err)
-		}
-
-		// Detect embedded subtitles
-		embeddedSubs := ffmpeg.DetectEmbeddedSubtitles(realPath, fileInfo.ModTime())
-
-		// Find the subtitle track by name
-		var streamIndex *int
-		for _, sub := range embeddedSubs {
-			if sub.Name == name {
-				streamIndex = sub.Index
-				break
-			}
-		}
-
-		if streamIndex == nil {
+		if track.Index == nil {
 			return http.StatusNotFound, fmt.Errorf("embedded subtitle track '%s' not found", name)
 		}
-
-		content, err = ffmpeg.ExtractSubtitleContent(realPath, *streamIndex)
+		content, err = ffmpeg.ExtractSubtitleContent(fileInfo.RealPath, *track.Index)
 		if err != nil {
 			return http.StatusInternalServerError, fmt.Errorf("failed to extract embedded subtitle: %v", err)
 		}
```

### Fix summary

The patch refactors the file path resolution for subtitles and lyrics. Instead of directly using `idx.GetRealPath` and then `filepath.Join` with user-controlled input, it now uses `files.FileInfoFaster` to obtain a validated `fileInfo.RealPath`. For subtitles, it then constructs the subtitle path using `filepath.Join(filepath.Dir(fileInfo.RealPath), filepath.Base(track.Name))`, ensuring that only the base name of the subtitle track is appended, preventing directory traversal.

## GHSA-88fw-v6x4-3f58 · org.springframework.data:spring-data-commons

2026-07-31 · Java · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The application used an unbounded ConcurrentHashMap to cache 'PersistentPropertyPath' objects, keyed by externally supplied property path strings. An attacker could provide a large number of unique property path strings, causing the cache to grow indefinitely.

### Impact

An attacker could exhaust available memory, leading to an OutOfMemoryError and effectively causing a denial of service for the application.

### Key diff

```
-	private final Map<TypeAndPath, PathResolution> propertyPaths = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
+	private final ConcurrentLruCache<TypeAndPath, PathResolution> propertyPaths = new ConcurrentLruCache<>(512, it -> createPersistentPropertyPath(it.path(), it.type()));
```

### Fix summary

The unbounded ConcurrentHashMap was replaced with a ConcurrentLruCache with a fixed size limit of 512 entries. This prevents the cache from growing indefinitely, mitigating the memory exhaustion vulnerability.

## GHSA-93wv-jw9v-4972 · io.netty:netty-codec-http2

2026-07-31 · Java · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The vulnerability in Netty's HTTP/2 decompression occurred because a ByteBuf reference count was not properly released when the decompressor channel was already closed. This led to unreleased direct memory, which could accumulate over time.

### Impact

An attacker could trigger a direct memory leak, leading to eventual OutOfMemory (OOM) errors and a Denial of Service (DoS) for the application using Netty, by continuously sending requests that cause the decompressor channel to close prematurely without proper resource deallocation.

### Key diff

```
--- a/codec-stomp/src/main/java/io/netty/handler/codec/stomp/StompSubframeEncoder.java
+++ b/codec-stomp/src/main/java/io/netty/handler/codec/stomp/StompSubframeEncoder.java
@@ -105,7 +106,12 @@ protected void encode(ChannelHandlerContext ctx, StompSubframe msg, List<Object>
         } else if (msg instanceof StompHeadersSubframe) {
             StompHeadersSubframe stompHeadersSubframe = (StompHeadersSubframe) msg;
             ByteBuf buf = ctx.alloc().buffer(headersSubFrameSize(stompHeadersSubframe));
-            encodeHeaders(stompHeadersSubframe, buf);
+            try {
+                encodeHeaders(stompHeadersSubframe, buf);
+            } catch (Exception e) {
+                buf.release();
+                PlatformDependent.throwException(e);
+            }
 
             out.add(convertHeadersSubFrame(stompHeadersSubframe, buf));
         } else if (msg instanceof StompContentSubframe) {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces `try-catch` blocks around the `encodeHeaders` calls in `StompSubframeEncoder` to ensure that the allocated `ByteBuf` is released if an exception occurs during header encoding. Additionally, it adds validation for illegal characters and empty header names in STOMP headers, preventing malformed input from causing issues and ensuring proper resource handling.

## GHSA-98pp-vccm-qm25 · redaxo/source

2026-07-31 · PHP · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `UNCLASSIFIED`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the `isAllowedExtension` function in Redaxo's Mediapool only checked for blocked extensions at the very end of the filename or in a specific double-extension pattern (e.g., `.php.ext`). It failed to account for multi-segment filenames like `foo.php.any.jpg`, which some web servers (especially Apache with `mod_php`) might still interpret and execute as PHP, effectively bypassing the intended security control.

### Impact

An authenticated attacker could upload a specially crafted file with a multi-segment filename containing a blocked extension (like `.php`) and an allowed extension, leading to remote code execution on the server.

### Key diff

```
--- a/redaxo/src/addons/mediapool/lib/mediapool.php
+++ b/redaxo/src/addons/mediapool/lib/mediapool.php
@@ -114,13 +114,13 @@ public static function isAllowedExtension(string $filename, array $args = []): b
             return false;
         }
 
+        // A blocked extension must not appear as a dot-separated segment anywhere in the filename,
+        // to prevent double/multi extension vulnerabilities: some webspaces execute a file named
+        // e.g. `foo.php.txt` or `foo.php.any.jpg` as php. Matching whole segments (instead of a
+        // substring) avoids false positives like `foo.json` (contains `.js`) or `js_datei.txt`.
         $blockedExtensions = self::getBlockedExtensions();
-        foreach ($blockedExtensions as $blockedExtension) {
-            // $blockedExtensions extensions are not allowed within filenames, to prevent double extension vulnerabilities:
-            // -> some webspaces execute files named file.php.txt as php
-            if (str_ends_with($filename, '.' . $blockedExtension) // Prüfe ob der String mit der verbotenen Endung endet
-                || str_ends_with($filename, '.' . $blockedExtension . '.' . $fileExt) // prüfe ob es keine doppelte Endung der Form *.php.ext gibt
-            ) {
+        foreach (explode('.', mb_strtolower($filename)) as $segment) {
+            if (in_array($segment, $blockedExtensions, true)) {
                 return false;
             }
         }
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `isAllowedExtension` function to split the filename into all its dot-separated segments. It then checks if any of these segments match a blocked extension, ensuring that even multi-segment filenames cannot bypass the extension filtering, thus preventing the execution of malicious files.

## GHSA-p7w7-4929-vpj5 · @dynatrace-oss/dynatrace-mcp-server

2026-07-31 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `MISSING_AUTH→ENDPOINT`

### Root cause

The Dynatrace MCP Server, when run as an HTTP server, did not enforce any authentication by default. This allowed any unauthenticated client to invoke internal tools and capabilities exposed by the server.

### Impact

An attacker could invoke any of the server's exposed tools and capabilities without authentication, potentially leading to unauthorized data access, modification, or execution of sensitive operations.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/index.ts
+++ b/src/index.ts
@@ -1554,6 +1562,24 @@ You can now execute new Grail queries (DQL, etc.) again. If this happens more of
         return;
       }
 
+      // Bearer token authentication: only enforced when MCP_BEARER_TOKEN is set
+      if (bearerToken) {
+        if (!validateBearerToken(req.headers.authorization, bearerToken)) {
+          res.writeHead(401, {
+            'WWW-Authenticate': 'Bearer realm="dynatrace-mcp"',
+            'Content-Type': 'application/json',
+          });
+          res.end(
+            JSON.stringify({
+              jsonrpc: '2.0',
+              id: null,
+              error: { code: -32001, message: 'Unauthorized' },
+            }),
+          );
+          return;
+        }
+      }
+
       // Parse request body for POST requests
       let body: unknown;
       // Create a fresh MCP server instance per request to support concurrent
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces an optional bearer token authentication mechanism. If the `MCP_BEARER_TOKEN` environment variable is set, all incoming HTTP requests (except health checks) are now validated against this token using a constant-time comparison. Additionally, a maximum request body size limit and a concurrent connection limit were added to mitigate potential DoS attacks.

## GHSA-phj3-59pf-cp83 · thumbor

2026-07-31 · Python · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `DOS→RESOURCE_EXHAUSTION`

### Root cause

The `proportion` filter in Thumbor allowed an attacker to specify an unbounded value for resizing an image after transformation. If a large or invalid proportion value was provided, it could lead to excessive resource consumption during image processing.

### Impact

An attacker could send specially crafted requests with invalid proportion values, causing the Thumbor server to consume excessive CPU and memory, leading to a denial of service for legitimate users.

### Key diff

```
--- a/thumbor/filters/proportion.py
+++ b/thumbor/filters/proportion.py
@@ -13,6 +13,9 @@
 class Filter(BaseFilter):
     @filter_method(BaseFilter.DecimalNumber)
     async def proportion(self, value):
+        if value <= 0 or value > 1.0:
+            return
+
         source_width, source_height = self.context.request.engine.size
 
         new_width = source_width * value
```

### Fix summary

The patch adds validation to the `proportion` filter. It now checks if the input `value` is within a valid range (greater than 0 and less than or equal to 1.0). If the value is outside this range, the filter method returns immediately, preventing the unbounded resize operation.

## GHSA-rc52-c4hv-w89p · sylius/mollie-plugin

2026-07-31 · PHP · **HIGH** 7.5 · Pattern: `MISSING_VERIFICATION→SIGNATURE`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because the payment webhook endpoint did not sufficiently verify the authenticity of the incoming payment status updates. Specifically, it relied on an 'orderId' from the request to fetch the order and then processed the Mollie payment status without cross-referencing the Mollie payment ID stored with the order against the ID provided in the webhook payload. This allowed an attacker to potentially forge payment status updates for any order by knowing its ID.

### Impact

An attacker could manipulate the payment status of an order, potentially marking an unpaid order as paid, leading to financial loss for the merchant or unauthorized access to products/services.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Controller/Shop/PaymentWebhookController.php
+++ b/src/Controller/Shop/PaymentWebhookController.php
@@ -107,6 +107,19 @@ public function __invoke(Request $request): Response
             return new JsonResponse(Response::HTTP_OK);
         }
 
+        $details = $payment->getDetails();
+        $storedMollieId = $details['payment_mollie_id'] ?? $order->getMolliePaymentId();
+        if ($storedMollieId !== $molliePayment->id) {
+            $this->logger?->addLog(sprintf(
+                'Mollie Webhook: payment ID mismatch for order %s. Expected %s, got %s.',
+                $request->get('orderId'),
+                (string) $storedMollieId,
+                $molliePayment->id,
+            ));
+
+            return new JsonResponse(Response::HTTP_OK);
+        }
+
         if (null !== $this->stateMachine && null !== $this->entityManager) {
             $this->applyTransition($payment, $molliePayment->status);
         } else {
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a crucial check in the payment webhook controller. It now verifies that the 'payment_mollie_id' stored in the order's details matches the 'id' provided in the Mollie payment object from the webhook. Additionally, the system now uses 'orderToken' instead of 'orderId' for fetching orders in some shop controllers, enhancing security by using a less predictable identifier.

## GHSA-mj3g-7xcc-x4vh · @phun-ky/defaults-deep

2026-07-31 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.3 · Pattern: `PROTOTYPE_POLLUTION→OVERRIDE`

### Root cause

The library's recursive merge function did not properly validate or sanitize property keys during object merging. This allowed an attacker to inject or modify properties of `Object.prototype` by providing specially crafted input containing keys like `__proto__`, `constructor`, or `prototype`.

### Impact

An attacker could inject arbitrary properties into `Object.prototype`, potentially leading to denial of service, remote code execution in certain contexts, or property tampering across all objects in the application.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/utils/merge-with.ts
+++ b/src/utils/merge-with.ts
@@ -155,6 +155,8 @@ export function mergeWith(
   return object as any;
 }
 
+const UNSAFE_KEYS = new Set<string>(['__proto__', 'constructor', 'prototype']);
+
 /**
  * Internal recursive merge implementation used by {@link mergeWith}.
  *
@@ -172,6 +174,9 @@ const baseMerge = (
   if (!isObjectLoose(source)) return;
 
   for (const key of ownKeys(source)) {
+    if (typeof key === 'string' && UNSAFE_KEYS.has(key)) {
+      continue;
+    }
     const srcValue = (source as any)[key];
     const objValue = (target as any)[key];
     const customized = customizer?.(
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a blacklist of unsafe keys (`__proto__`, `constructor`, `prototype`) that are explicitly skipped during the merging process. This prevents an attacker from modifying global object prototypes through crafted input.

## GHSA-rxcw-mc6f-6hr3 · jodit

2026-07-31 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 7.2 · Pattern: `UNSANITIZED_INPUT→XSS`

### Root cause

The Jodit editor's HTML sanitization logic did not correctly handle HTML content 'smuggled' inside MathML or SVG tags. Specifically, rawtext carriers like `<style>` or `<mglyph>` within `<math>` or `<svg>` elements could bypass the sanitizer. When the browser reparses the DOM, these 'smuggled' HTML elements could be hoisted out of their foreign content context and become live, executable nodes in the main HTML document.

### Impact

An attacker could inject malicious JavaScript code into the editor's output. When a user views or interacts with the sanitized HTML, the script would execute in their browser, leading to session hijacking, data theft, or defacement.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/core/helpers/html/safe-html.ts
+++ b/src/core/helpers/html/safe-html.ts
@@ -29,6 +66,16 @@ export function safeHTML(
 		return;
 	}
 
+	// Drop HTML smuggled into MathML/SVG before the walk: a reparse would otherwise hoist its hidden
+	// markup into a live node.
+	const foreign = box.querySelectorAll('math *, svg *');
+
+	for (let i = 0; i < foreign.length; i++) {
+		if (foreign[i].isConnected && isSmuggledForeignHtml(foreign[i])) {
+			Dom.safeRemove(foreign[i]);
+		}
+	}
+
 	const removeEvents = options.removeEventAttributes ?? options.removeOnError;
 
 	// Single synchronous traversal of the subtree. Besides removing event
```

### Fix summary

The patch introduces a new function, `isSmuggledForeignHtml`, to identify HTML elements that are incorrectly placed within MathML/SVG outside of legitimate integration points. Before the main sanitization walk, the `safeHTML` function now proactively queries for all `math` and `svg` children and removes any identified 'smuggled' HTML elements, preventing them from being re-parsed and activated.

> ⚡ **Pattern match — 90 occurrences**

## GHSA-5846-7qm3-r52j · dssrf

2026-07-31 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `SSRF→INTERNAL_ACCESS`

### Root cause

The original `resolve_all_records` function only used `dns.resolve4` and `dns.resolve6` to resolve hostnames. If a hostname did not have A or AAAA records but could still be resolved via `dns.lookup` (e.g., due to local DNS configurations or specific network setups), the application would fail to identify its internal IP status, leading to a bypass of the SSRF protection.

### Impact

An attacker could potentially bypass the Server-Side Request Forgery (SSRF) protection by using hostnames that resolve to internal IP addresses through `dns.lookup` but not through `dns.resolve4` or `dns.resolve6`, thereby accessing internal resources.

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/helpers.ts
+++ b/src/helpers.ts
@@ -270,17 +270,24 @@ export function is_ip_internal(ip: string): boolean {
 import { promises as dns } from "dns";
 
 async function resolve_all_records(host: string) {
-    const A = await dns.resolve4(host).catch(() => []);
-    const AAAA = await dns.resolve6(host).catch(() => []);
-    const CNAME = await dns.resolveCname(host).catch(() => []);
-
-    return {
-        A,
-        AAAA,
-        CNAME
-    };
+    const [A, AAAA, CNAME] = await Promise.all([
+        dns.resolve4(host).catch(() => [] as string[]),
+        dns.resolve6(host).catch(() => [] as string[]),
+        dns.resolveCname(host).catch(() => [] as string[])
+    ]);
+
+    if (A.length === 0 && AAAA.length === 0) {
+        try {
+            const lookups = await dns.lookup(host, { all: true });
+            for (const entry of lookups) {
+                if (entry.family === 4) A.push(entry.address);
+                if (entry.family === 6) AAAA.push(entry.address);
+            }
+        } catch {}
+    }
 
+    return { A, AAAA, CNAME };
 }
```

### Fix summary

The patch modifies the `resolve_all_records` function to include a fallback mechanism. If `dns.resolve4` and `dns.resolve6` return no records, it now attempts to use `dns.lookup` to resolve the hostname, ensuring that all possible IP addresses associated with a host are considered for internal IP checks.

## GHSA-cj54-hpcc-gj6h · thumbor

2026-07-31 · Python · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The vulnerability existed because Thumbor performed URL decoding of the path after validating it against the root path. An attacker could use percent-encoded path traversal sequences (e.g., %2e%2e for ..) which would bypass the initial `abspath` and `startswith` checks, and then be decoded later, allowing access to arbitrary files outside the intended root directory.

### Impact

An attacker could read arbitrary files on the server's file system, potentially leading to sensitive information disclosure, such as configuration files, source code, or system files like /etc/passwd.

### Key diff

```
--- a/thumbor/loaders/file_loader.py
+++ b/thumbor/loaders/file_loader.py
@@ -19,11 +19,16 @@
 async def load(context, path):
     root = abspath(context.config.FILE_LOADER_ROOT_PATH)
 
-    file_path = join(
-        context.config.FILE_LOADER_ROOT_PATH.rstrip("/"), path.lstrip("/")
-    )
-    file_path = abspath(file_path)
-    inside_root_path = file_path.startswith(
-        abspath(context.config.FILE_LOADER_ROOT_PATH)
-    )
+    # Decode percent-encoding BEFORE the security check.
+    # encoded path would allow sequences like %2e%2e to pass the
+    # abspath/startswith guard and later be decoded into real traversal
+    # segments (e.g. unquote("root/%2e%2e/etc/passwd") -> "root/../etc/passwd").
+    file_path = abspath(join(root, unquote(path).lstrip("/")))
+    inside_root_path = _inside_root_path(root, file_path)
 
     result = LoaderResult()
```

### Fix summary

The patch ensures that the URL path is unquoted (percent-decoded) *before* it is joined with the root path and validated. A new helper function `_inside_root_path` using `commonpath` is introduced for more robust path validation. A backward compatibility check is maintained for literal (non-decoded) paths, but only after ensuring they also remain within the root.

## GHSA-g65v-27r3-5p6m · guard-livereload

2026-07-31 · Ruby · **MODERATE** 0.0 · Pattern: `PATH_TRAVERSAL→FILE_READ`

### Root cause

The application allowed direct file serving based on user-supplied paths without proper validation. Specifically, the `_serve` method would call `_serve_file(path)` if `_readable_file(path)` returned true, making it vulnerable to path traversal sequences like `../`.

### Impact

An attacker could read arbitrary files on the server's filesystem that the application had read access to, potentially leading to sensitive information disclosure.

### Key diff

```
--- a/lib/guard/livereload/websocket.rb
+++ b/lib/guard/livereload/websocket.rb
@@ -58,8 +61,8 @@ def _livereload_js_path
 
       def _serve(path)
         return _serve_file(_livereload_js_path) if path == './livereload.js'
-        return _serve_file(path) if _readable_file(path)
-        send_data("HTTP/1.1 404 Not Found\r\nContent-Type: text/plain\r\nContent-Length: 13\r\n\r\n404 Not Found")
+        data = _readable_file(path) ? HTTP_DATA_FORBIDDEN : HTTP_DATA_NOT_FOUND
+        send_data(data)
         close_connection_after_writing
       end
```

### Fix summary

The patch removes the ability to serve arbitrary files based on the `path` parameter. Instead of attempting to serve the file, it now returns a 403 Forbidden response if the file is readable but not the specifically allowed `livereload.js` file, and a 404 Not Found otherwise.

## GHSA-g956-2f74-rmv7 · hashi-vault-js

2026-07-31 · JavaScript · **HIGH** 0.0 · Pattern: `UNCLASSIFIED`

### Root cause

The application was constructing URLs by directly concatenating user-controlled input (like 'type', 'format', 'role_name', 'username', 'group', 'appRole') into the URL path and query string without proper encoding. This allowed special characters in the input to alter the intended URL structure.

### Impact

An attacker could manipulate the URL path to access unintended API endpoints or resources (path traversal) or inject arbitrary query parameters to modify the request's behavior or bypass security checks (query parameter injection).

### Key diff

```
--- a/src/Vault.js
+++ b/src/Vault.js
@@ -271,7 +271,7 @@ export default class Vault {
     let message = {};
     if (config.sysCounterTypes.includes(type)) {
       const Options = {
-        url: `${config.sysInternalCounters}/${type}`,
+        url: `${config.sysInternalCounters}/${encodeURIComponent(type)}`,
         method: 'get',
         headers: {
           "X-Vault-Token": sudoToken
@@ -307,7 +307,8 @@ export default class Vault {
       }
     };
     if (config.sysMetricFormats.includes(format)) {
-      Options.url= `${config.sysMetrics}?format=${format}`;
+      const qs = new URLSearchParams({ format }).toString();
+      Options.url= `${config.sysMetrics}?${qs}`;
     }
```

### Fix summary

The patch addresses the vulnerability by applying URL encoding to all user-controlled variables that are incorporated into the URL path using `encodeURIComponent()`. For query parameters, it now uses `URLSearchParams` to safely construct the query string, ensuring that special characters are properly escaped.
